⏺ The Expo app is set up with NativeWind and a Next.js-style folder structure,
  and AI logging is in place from the start. It hasn't been run on a simulator
  or device yet. Typecheck and lint pass with no errors or warnings, the iOS
  bundle builds with the Tailwind classes compiled in, and expo-doctor passes
  all 21 checks.

  Stack
  - Expo SDK 57, Expo Router and TypeScript.
  - NativeWind 4.2.7 with Tailwind CSS 3.4. I checked the NativeWind docs first:
    this is the current stable setup for Expo 57.
  - I also added a cn() helper for combining class names, and ESLint, which the
    template didn't include.

  Folder structure (same ideas as the Next.js App Router)
  src/
  ├── app/                  # routes only
  │   ├── _layout.tsx       # ≈ layout.tsx (loads the global CSS)
  │   ├── +not-found.tsx    # ≈ not-found.tsx
  │   └── (tabs)/           # route group: _layout.tsx, index.tsx (Home),
  settings.tsx
  ├── components/ui/        # Button
  ├── components/layout/    # Screen (keeps content clear of notches and status
  bar)
  ├── lib/utils.ts          # cn()
  ├── constants/  hooks/  types/
  └── global.css
  I removed the template's demo screens, components and images. README.md has a
  table matching each Next.js file name to its Expo Router equivalent.

  AI logs
  - ai-logs/README.md explains the naming format and lists every session.
  - ai-logs/2026-09-27-01-project-setup.md has your prompt word for word and a
    summary of what I did.
  - I added the logging rule to AGENTS.md so later sessions keep following it. I
    also saved it to memory, so future sessions will remind you to export.

❯ so the command to start the expo app is npx expo start??? right???

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes, npx expo start is the command. npm start does exactly the same thing,
  because the start script in package.json runs expo start.

  Once it's running:
  - Press i for the iOS simulator, a for the Android emulator, or w for web.
  - Or scan the QR code with the Expo Go app on your phone.

  If a style change doesn't show up after you edit tailwind.config.js or
  babel.config.js, restart with a cleared cache: npx expo start -c.

  I've added this question and answer to the session log in
  ai-logs/2026-09-27-01-project-setup.md.

❯ # AI Dream Journal — Product Context

  ## Assignment

  Build a polished **AI Dream Journal mobile app** within 24 hours.

  Requirements:
  - Expo + React Native
  - Supabase
  - Must run on phone or simulator
  - Must use AI for:
    - Text
    - Images
    - Audio
  - AI coding sessions must be saved as `.md` files inside `ai-logs/`
  - Remove API keys/secrets from logs before pushing

  Reference for UI quality and visual direction:
  - Pillowtalk
  - We will provide reference screenshots/images separately

  Before designing screens, analyze the provided reference images, understand
  their design system, and then derive our screens according to that style
  rather than directly copying them.

  ---

  # Product Idea

  The app is a **voice-first AI dream journal**.

  The user wakes up, records or writes a dream, and the app:

  - Transcribes the dream
  - Generates a title
  - Detects emotions
  - Detects important symbols/themes
  - Generates dream artwork
  - Asks one meaningful reflection question
  - Saves the dream
  - Finds connections with previous dreams

  The app should feel emotional, calm, visual, premium, and personal.

  It should not feel like a generic AI chatbot or notes app.

  ---

  # USP — Dream Echo

  Our main differentiator is:

  > **Most dream journals interpret one dream. Our app remembers your dream
  world.**

  The app detects recurring things across previous dreams, such as:

  - Symbols
  - People
  - Places
  - Emotions
  - Themes

  Example:

  > **Dream Echo**
  > Water has appeared in 3 of your previous dreams.

  This should be one of the key product moments.

  ---

  # Core User Flow

  ```text
  Open App
     ↓
  Record / Write Dream
     ↓
  AI Processing
     ↓
  Dream Artwork + Analysis
     ↓
  AI Reflection Question
     ↓
  Dream Echo
     ↓
  Save to Dream History
  ```

  ---

  # Screens to Develop

  ## 1. Onboarding

  Short 2–3 screen introduction.

  Focus on:
  - Remembering dreams
  - Understanding recurring patterns
  - Discovering your dream world

  ---

  ## 2. Home

  Main entry screen.

  Primary CTA:

  > What do you remember?

  Large voice recording action.

  Also show:
  - Write instead
  - Recent dreams
  - Recent Dream Echo if available

  ---

  ## 3. Dream Recording

  Immersive voice capture screen.

  Show:
  - Recording state
  - Audio visualization
  - Timer
  - Finish action

  Keep it simple and distraction-free.

  ---

  ## 4. AI Processing

  A polished transition screen.

  Example:

  > Remembering your dream...

  Possible stages:

  - Understanding the story
  - Finding emotions
  - Finding symbols
  - Painting your dream

  ---

  ## 5. Dream Detail / Reveal

  This is the main "wow" screen.

  Show:
  - AI-generated dream artwork
  - Dream title
  - Date
  - Emotions
  - Symbols/themes
  - Dream transcript or summary

  This screen should be highly visual.

  ---

  ## 6. AI Reflection

  Show one thoughtful question based on the dream.

  Example:

  > The house felt familiar in your dream. Does it remind you of somewhere from
  your childhood?

  User can answer by voice or text.

  This can be part of Dream Detail if it fits the reference design better.

  ---

  ## 7. Dream Echo

  Show recurring patterns from previous dreams.

  Example:

  > **Dream Echo**
  > Water appeared in 2 previous dreams.

  Allow users to open related dreams.

  This is the main USP.

  ---

  ## 8. Dream History

  A visual gallery of previous dreams.

  Use generated artwork as the main visual element.

  Possible layout:
  - Cards
  - Gallery
  - Timeline

  Choose the layout after analyzing the reference screenshots.

  ---

  # Optional Screen

  ## Dream Patterns

  Only build if time allows.

  Show simple patterns such as:

  - Total dreams
  - Most common emotion
  - Most recurring symbol
  - Most recurring theme

  Do not turn this into a complex analytics dashboard.

  ---

  # Design Direction

  Use Pillowtalk as the quality benchmark.

  Analyze reference screenshots for:

  - Typography
  - Spacing
  - Colors
  - Gradients
  - Cards
  - Border radius
  - Navigation
  - Buttons
  - Visual hierarchy
  - Motion
  - Content density

  Then re-evaluate our screens and derive the final layouts accordingly.

  Do not blindly copy Pillowtalk.

  Our design should feel more:
  - Dream-like
  - Atmospheric
  - Calm
  - Surreal
  - Emotional

  ---

  # Product Principles

  1. Voice-first experience
  2. Minimal steps
  3. AI should feel embedded, not like ChatGPT
  4. Dream artwork should be visually important
  5. Show one meaningful insight instead of too much analysis
  6. Dream Echo should communicate long-term memory
  7. Polish is more important than number of features

  ---

  # Do Not Prioritize

  Avoid:
  - Social features
  - Payments
  - Subscriptions
  - Sleep tracking
  - Lucid dreaming courses
  - Dream dictionary
  - Complex analytics
  - Community features
  - Complex settings
  - Complicated graph visualizations

  ---

  # Final Product Positioning

  ## Dream Echo

  **A voice-first AI dream journal that turns dreams into visual memories and
  remembers what keeps returning.**

  Core experience:

  > **Speak → Visualize → Reflect → Connect**

  Core USP:

  > **Most dream journals interpret one dream. Dream Echo remembers your dream
  world.**

⏺ I've gone through all 27 screenshots and written the screen-by-screen spec in
  docs/SCREENS.md. That's the file we'll build the frontend from, one screen at
  a time.

  Other files I added
  - docs/PRODUCT.md: your product brief, saved word for word.
  - docs/reference/: the screenshots as R01–R27. The spec cites them by these
    numbers.
  - The session log now includes this prompt and a summary of the work.

  What's in SCREENS.md
  1. What each screenshot shows and where it fits in our app.
  2. Design system: colors, the dark navy background and the lavender one used
     on the profile and settings screens, fonts, spacing, corner rounding, icons
     and animation.
  3. App flow: a diagram of how the screens connect, plus a route for each
     screen.
  4. Data model: the Dream and DreamEcho data shapes, and how a dream moves from
     Record → Processing → Reveal → Echo → Archive.
  5. Shared components: about 25 reusable pieces (bottom action bar, dream card,
     Echo card, symbol icon with its x3 count badge, insight card, waveform and
     so on).
  6. Screens S1–S10: layout from top to bottom, components, data, empty and
     error states, where each tap goes, and a build checklist for each.
  7. Assets checklist, build order and open questions.

  Where our app differs from the reference
  - No tab bar. The reference uses a floating three-button bar at the bottom
    instead, so I planned a single stack of screens. When we start building,
    I'll remove the Home/Settings tabs from the starter.
  - AI artwork is the main visual. The reference is text only; ours puts the
    dream's artwork at the top of the Reveal screen and uses it in the Archive.
  - Dream Echo gets more space. The reference only shows a small x2 badge on
    symbols. We add an Echo card on Home and Reveal, a dedicated Echo screen
    that lists the related dreams, and a "World" view of the symbol map in the
    Archive.
  - Left out: archetypes, the cultural/Bible quotes, the film-hero cards and
    widgets. These make it a dream dictionary, which your brief rules out.

  Decisions I need from you (listed at the end of SCREENS.md)
  - Fonts: are you providing them? Until then I've suggested Instrument Serif
    and Manrope, which are close to the reference.
  - App name: "Dream Echo" or "Dreamify"?
  - Transcription: show the words live while recording, or transcribe after the
    user finishes? After is simpler for v1.
  - Audio AI: besides speech-to-text, should the app read the reflection
    question aloud? That would make the audio AI requirement more visible.

  assets/Dreamify.zip is 16 MB and doesn't need to be in the repo now that the
  screenshots are in docs/reference/. I suggest deleting it or adding it to
  .gitignore.

  When you send the images, icons and fonts, I'll fill in the assets checklist
  and start building the shared components and Home.

  Tips for exporting

  - SVGs: use strokes, not filled shapes, with a white stroke color, and no
    hidden layers or embedded images. I'll load them with react-native-svg, so
    they can be recolored and resized.
  - PNGs: export at 3× (moon@3x.png) if you can. If not, one large file is fine.
  - Missing assets: if something isn't ready, say so and I'll use a placeholder,
    so it won't block the build.

⏺ I've updated docs/SCREENS.md to use Bricolage Grotesque for headings and
  important text and the system font (SF Pro on iOS, Roboto on Android) for
  paragraphs, labels and dates. I also logged this prompt in the session file.

  Bricolage has no italic. The reference highlights key words in a question with
  italics ("have you feared"), so I changed how we emphasize them: the question
  is set in a light, slightly dimmed weight, and the key words are bold and
  full white. Example: "What part of yourself have you feared, but may now be
  ready to hear?"

  How the two fonts are split:
  - Bricolage: screen titles, dream titles, the reflection question, Dream Echo
    messages, emotion and theme lists, dream card previews and buttons. At large
    sizes it's set with tight letter spacing so it keeps the reference's
    condensed feel.
  - System font: the dream text itself (the transcript, the interpretation and
    the insight answer) at a large, easy-to-read size, plus normal paragraphs,
    labels and dates.

  Bricolage comes from a Google Fonts package, so you don't need to send any
  font files. Send the moon, jellyfish, symbol icons and anything else from the
  list when you're ready. Once they arrive I'll start on the shared components
  and Screen 1.

⏺ I drew 30 symbol icons and saved them in assets/icons/symbols/. The moon and
  jellyfish are ready to use: both have transparent backgrounds and are large
  enough.

  The icons
  - They're white line art in the same style as the reference, one SVG per
    symbol. You can see them all inside the reference-style circle in
    docs/reference/symbols-preview.jpg.
  - They can be recolored in code, so they also work on the light Patterns and
    Settings screens.
  - I checked the preview and redrew three that were too thin: snake, key and
    bird.

  The 30 symbols are:

  Nature: water, ocean, fire, desert, forest, mountain, grass, cloud, wind,
  snow,
  sun, moon
  Creatures: wolf, crow, cat, snake, bird, web
  Places & things: door, house, stairs, train, mirror, key
  Abstract: light, heart, death, eye, mask, star

  How they connect to the AI: each file name is the symbol name the AI will have
  to return, for example water, so every detected symbol gets an icon
  automatically. If the AI returns a symbol that isn't in the list, the app
  shows star instead. The list is in assets/icons/symbols/README.md.

❯ THese icons are all black what about white color icons???

⏺ They look black because they use currentColor, and editors and file previews
  treat that as black. I'll set the stroke to white in the files themselves. In
  the app we can still pass a different stroke to recolor them (for example on
  the light Patterns screen), because the color is set once on the outer <svg>
  element and nowhere else.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ All 30 icons in assets/icons/symbols/ are now white. Before, they used
  currentColor, which editors and file previews show as black.

  The white is set only on the outer <svg> element, so we can still recolor an
  icon in code if we need to, for example dark icons on the light Patterns
  screen.

  On a white background, such as the VS Code file preview or Finder, they'll
  look invisible now; they show up fine on the app's dark screens. You can see
  them on the dark preview sheet at docs/reference/symbols-preview.jpg.

  I also updated the icon README and the session log.

⏺ Screen 2 — Home

  The screen you land on every morning after onboarding. There's one obvious
  action (record a dream), plus a sense that the app remembers your earlier
  dreams.

  Layout, top to bottom (over the night-sky background):

  1. Header: "Good morning, name" on the left and a round profile avatar on the
     right. Tapping the avatar opens Patterns later.
  2. Main area:
     - Today's date, small ("Saturday, 27 September")
     - The large headline "What do you remember?"
     - Record orb: a big white circle with a mic, slowly pulsing with a soft
       glowing ring. Tapping it opens Recording.
     - A "Write instead" link under it, which opens the same screen in typing
       mode
  3. Dream Echo card (our main selling point): a symbol icon with an x3 badge,
     the "Dream Echo" label, and a line like "Water has appeared in 3 of your
     dreams." Tapping it opens the Echo screen.
  4. Recent dreams: a sideways-scrolling row of 3–5 cards, each showing the
     dream's artwork, its date and its title. "See all" opens the Archive.
  5. Bottom action bar (the three-button style from the reference): an archive
     button · a Search pill · a write button.

  Other states:
  - No dreams yet: the Echo card and Recent dreams are hidden, and a quiet line
    appears instead: "Your first dream starts your dream world."
  - Dream still being processed: a "Painting your dream…" card appears first in
    Recent dreams.

  For now the screen runs on sample dreams. Supabase comes later.

  about 1024 px wide, JPG. Themes that match our symbols work best: water, a
  door in a desert, a wolf, a forest, a mirror, a staircase, the ocean, a
  train. Put them in assets/images/dreams/.

  Dream artwork prompts

  Settings: portrait 4:5 (for example 1024×1280), JPG. Save them as
  assets/images/dreams/dream-01.jpg to dream-08.jpg.

  Add this style to the end of every prompt (it's also the style the AI will use
  for real dream art later, so everything matches):
  surreal dreamscape, painterly digital art, deep midnight navy and indigo
  palette with soft silver moonlight, subtle cold blue glow, atmospheric haze
  and mist, fine film grain, quiet and calm but slightly uncanny, minimal
  composition with lots of negative space, cinematic wide depth, soft focus
  edges, muted desaturated tones, no text
  Negative prompt (if your tool has one): text, watermark, logo, bright
  saturated colors, neon, cartoon, anime, 3d render look, close-up faces, gore,
  horror, clutter, border

  #: 01 Door in the Black Desert
  Prompt: A single wooden door standing upright alone in an endless desert of
  black sand dunes, no walls around it, the door slightly open with a faint
  warm light behind it, ash-grey sky with a pale full moon, long soft shadows,
  a tiny lone figure in the distance walking toward it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 02 The Ocean That Disappeared
  Prompt: A vast dry seabed at night where the ocean has vanished, old wooden
  boats stranded and tilted on cracked sand, shallow puddles reflecting a huge
  pale moon, distant thin line of water on the horizon, mist drifting low

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  in 4 dreams, the moon in 3 and a door in 2. So Home can truthfully say "Water

⏺ The crash is fixed, and I confirmed it in the iOS simulator this time, not
  just in the browser.

  Cause: your Expo Go crash reports showed the app was force-closed by an error
  thrown on the animation thread.
  - When the morph finished, its end-of-animation callback tried to schedule the
    move to Record using a timer that only exists on the animation thread.
  - On a phone, an error on that thread kills the app instantly. That's the "orb
    freezes, then the app closes" you saw.
  - On web everything runs on one thread, so my earlier browser test didn't
    catch it.

  Fixes:
  1. Crash: the move to Record is now scheduled with a normal timer on the main
     JavaScript side, and it's cancelled if you leave Home first.
  2. Freezing risk: on phones the orb was re-rendering the whole component 60
     times a second. It now hands each frame straight to Skia, the drawing
     library, which is how Skia is meant to be used.
  3. A layout bug I spotted while testing: on the Record screen the back button
     sat on top of the clock. It now sits below the status bar.

  Simulator check: macOS doesn't allow scripted taps, so I added a temporary
  trigger that performs the same action as a tap. With it I confirmed in Expo
  Go:
  - the button turned into the orb, the orb animated, and Record opened;
  - the app stayed open, and no new crash report appeared.

  I then removed the temporary trigger. Typecheck and lint pass.

  The simulator was running from your dev server on port 8081, so your phone
  should pick up the fix too. If it doesn't, restart with npx expo start -c.

  I added the lessons to AGENTS.md: don't schedule timers or navigation from
  animation callbacks, and always check phone behaviour in the simulator. The
  session log is updated too.

      417  - The headline cross-fades to "I'm **listening**…" ("**Paused*
           *" when paused). The orb's `level` follows mic metering (dBFS 
           −55 → 0, −12 → 1), so it spins faster and swells with the voic
           e.

⏺ Screen 3 is now the Write screen, and it's built. Since recording moved onto
  Home, S3 is the "Write instead" path for typing a dream when you can't or
  don't want to speak.

  It opens from "Write instead" or the ✎ button on Home:
  - Top: today's date and time, "Your dream", and a live word count.
  - Writing area: a large text field that opens with the cursor ready. The
    placeholder reads "Start with anything you remember… a place, a feeling, a
    face."
  - Starter chips: "I was in… · There was… · I felt… · Someone… · Then… · I woke
    up…". Tapping one adds it to your text, which helps a half-awake mind get
    started. They hide after 12 words.
  - Bottom bar, which stays above the keyboard:
    - ✕ closes the screen, and asks "Discard this dream?" if you've written
      something;
    - 🎙 goes back to Home and starts recording, keeping what you've typed;
    - ✓ saves the dream. It unlocks at 3 words.
  - Autosaved draft: your text is saved as you type. If you leave, it's still
    there next time.
  - After ✓, Home shows "Dream saved", and the dream appears first in Recent
    with the small processing orb.

  Tested:
  - Chrome: every flow above works, including restoring the draft, switching to
    voice, and saving back to Home. There were no errors.
  - iOS simulator: the screen shows correctly with the cursor ready.
  - Not tested: the layout moving up above the on-screen keyboard. The simulator
    used the Mac keyboard, so please check that part on your phone.
  - Typecheck and lint pass.

  Along the way I fixed the row of chips stretching and pushing the buttons
  down, and removed a blue browser outline around the text field.

  The spec (S3 section, route table), README and session log are updated. The
  old /record placeholder is gone; the Write screen is at /write.

  Next: Screen 4, Processing. After you finish recording or writing, the orb
  changes shape through "understanding → emotions → symbols → painting" before
  the dream is revealed.

❯ NOw we have to build the forth screen....
⏺ Screen 4 is Processing: the moment between speaking or writing a dream and
  seeing it revealed. Here's the plan:

  - Visual: the orb over a faint moon glow, changing shape at each stage:
    working → composing → connecting → weaving.
  - Headline: "Remembering your dream…"
  - Four stages, each with a status dot (waiting → pulsing → ✓) and a small
    preview of its result as it completes:
    a. Understanding the story → the dream's title
    b. Finding emotions → "Unease · Wonder"
    c. Finding symbols → symbol icons, plus the Dream Echo line ("Water echoes 3
       of your dreams")
    d. Painting your dream → the artwork
  - When it's done: the orb gives way to the artwork card, the title appears,
    and a "Reveal your dream" button leads to S5.
  - "Continue in background": go back to Home while it keeps working. That means
    the processing has to run app-wide (in the provider), not inside the
    screen.

  There's no AI backend yet, so I'll write a mock pipeline with a real-looking
  interface. It will find symbols and emotions from the words you use, build a
  title and a question, and pick one of the sample artworks. When Supabase and
  the real AI are connected, only that one file changes.

  Starting with the data layer:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  confusion, I'll instead show the artwork with a "Reveal your dream" button,
  noting this deviation in the spec. Now writing the mock AI analyzer and
  pipeline.

⏺ Screen 4 (Processing) is built. It runs after you finish recording on Home,
  save a written dream, or tap a dream that's still processing in Recent.

  What you see:
  - The orb inside a faint moon, changing shape at each step: particles orbiting
    → a flowing band → a constellation → woven strands.
  - "Remembering your dream…" with four steps. Each gets a pulsing dot while it
    works, then a ✓ and a small preview of its result:
    a. Understanding the story → the title, e.g. "The Water and the Night"
    b. Finding emotions → Unease · Calm · Wonder
    c. Finding symbols → the symbol icons, plus the Dream Echo line: "Water
       echoes 4 of your dreams"
    d. Painting your dream → Your dream is ready
  - When it's done, the orb gives way to the artwork card, the title becomes the
    headline, and a "Reveal your dream" button appears. I used a button instead
    of the spec's automatic jump, so you choose when the reveal happens and
    never land unexpectedly on a placeholder page.
  - "Continue in background" takes you back to Home. Processing keeps going
    there, and the dream's card turns into its artwork when it finishes.

  The AI part is a placeholder for now. There's no backend yet, so a mock does
  the work:
  - It detects symbols and emotions from the words you use, then builds a title,
    themes, an interpretation and a reflection question.
  - It picks the best-matching sample artwork.
  - Voice dreams get a sample transcript, because speech-to-text isn't connected
    yet.

  When we connect Supabase and the real AI, only the file
  src/lib/ai/mock-analyzer.ts changes; the screens stay as they are.

  Tested:
  - Chrome: the steps and previews appear in order, "Reveal your dream" works,
    and "Continue in background" finishes on Home. No errors.
  - iOS simulator: a real recording flowed straight into Processing, and the orb
    changed shape with every step. I didn't catch the constellation shape in a
    screenshot, but the app kept running through that step.
  - Typecheck and lint pass.
  - Not tested: the "Try again" error screen, because the mock never fails.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec, README and session log are
  updated.

  Next: Screen 5, the Dream Reveal: full-screen artwork, emotions, symbols,
  interpretation, the Dream Echo card, and the reflection question with the
  pull-up insight card. "Reveal your dream" currently lands on its placeholder.

⏺ Screen 5, the Dream Reveal, is built. It's what "Reveal your dream" opens
  after Processing, and what you see when you tap any dream.

  Top to bottom:
  1. The artwork fills the top of the screen. It fades in from a blur, moves
     slightly as you scroll, and stretches if you pull down. The date and a
     large title sit over it.
  2. Emotions on the left, as a list (e.g. Fear / Recognition / Calm).
  3. Symbols ✦ in a sideways row of tiles. Each shows its icon, name and an x2 /
     x4 badge for how often it appeared in earlier dreams. Tapping a tile opens
     the Dream Echo screen.
  4. Interpretation: a short paragraph.
  5. Themes, as a list.
  6. The Dream Echo card, e.g. "Night has appeared in 2 of your previous
     dreams".
  7. ✶ The reflection question in large text, with key words in bold, e.g. "What
     part of yourself have you feared…?". Once you've answered it, your white
     Insight card appears under it with an Edit link.
  8. "Your dream": your own words, collapsed to 3 lines (tap to expand), with a
     play button for voice recordings.

  Buttons:
  - A new dream (arriving from Processing): ✕ discards it after a confirmation ·
    Add an insight · ✓ saves it and returns Home with "Saved to your dream
    world".
  - An older dream: just Add insight / Edit insight at the bottom, with back and
    share at the top.

  The insight card (answering the question): the page dims and a white card
  slides up with the cursor ready. You can type, or tap the mic to record a
  voice note; a small dark-dotted orb reacts while you speak. Tap ✓ to save, and
  your phone gives a small vibration. Drag the card down or tap outside it to
  close.

  Tested:
  - Chrome:
    - the wolf dream shows every section;
    - a symbol tile opens its Echo page;
    - the full new-dream flow works: write → process → reveal → add insight →
      save → ✓ → Home.
    - No errors.
  - iOS simulator: the screen and the insight sheet both work natively (I opened
    the sheet with a temporary trigger, since then removed).
  - Typecheck and lint pass.

  Not tested yet:
  - The insight card moving up above the on-screen keyboard (the simulator uses
    the Mac keyboard).
  - Recording a voice note on a real phone.
  - Voice notes are saved as audio, not converted to text. That comes with the
    AI backend.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec (S5/S5b checklists, with notes on
  what changed from the plan), README and session log are updated.

  Next: Screen 6, the Dream Echo page: how one symbol keeps returning across
  your dreams, with a list of those dreams. The symbol tiles and Echo cards
  already link to it.

       62 +function tally<T extends { key: string }>(items: T[]) {       
       63 +  const map = new Map<string, T & { count: number }>();       
       64 +  for (const item of items) {                                 
       65 +    const prev = map.get(item.key);                           
       66 +    map.set(item.key, { ...item, count: (prev?.count ?? 0) + 1
          + });                                                          
       67 +  }                                                           
       68 +  return [...map.values()].sort((a, b) => b.count - a.count); 
       69 +}                                                             
       70 +

          +d. */

⏺ Screen 6, Dream Echo, is built. It's the page that shows how one symbol keeps
  coming back across your dreams. It opens from the symbol tiles and Echo card
  on the Dream Reveal, from the Echo card on Home, and from the constellation on
  this page.

  Top to bottom:
  1. The symbol's name in large text ("Water") with its icon and an x4 badge,
     over a faint image from the latest dream that contains it.
  2. ✶ The key statement: "Water has appeared in 4 of your dreams since
     September", with a "First seen · 21 September" chip.
     - If it's appeared only once: "This is the first time Wolf has appeared.
       I'll let you know if it returns."
     - If it's never appeared: "Snake hasn't appeared in your dreams yet."
  3. What it may mean:
     - a one-line meaning ("Emotion, the unconscious, and change.");
     - a personal note written from your dreams: "In your dreams, water tends to
       arrive with peace and wonder, most recently in 'Jellyfish Over the
       Sleeping City'. It often arrives together with light…"
  4. Felt with water: the emotions from those dreams (Peace / Wonder /
     Uncertainty / Waiting).
  5. Dreams with water: compact cards with artwork, date, title and symbols. The
     dream you came from is highlighted as "this dream". Tapping one opens it.
  6. Often appears with: a small constellation with water in the middle and its
     companion symbols around it (Light, Houses, Train, Door, Mirror, Stars).
     Nodes are bigger and lines brighter when they appear together more often.
     Tapping one opens that symbol's Echo page.

  I kept the meanings to one line on purpose, because your brief says no dream
  dictionary. The focus is your pattern. The personal note is a template for
  now; the AI backend will write it later.

  The counts match Home: Home says "3 of your previous dreams", and this page
  says "4 of your dreams", which includes the newest one.

  Tested:
  - Chrome: the full Water page, tapping a constellation node (→ Light), tapping
    a dream card (→ that dream), and the "first time" and "never" messages. No
    errors.
  - iOS simulator: the page renders natively. The blue gear in the iOS
    screenshot is Expo Go's developer menu button, not part of the app.
  - Typecheck and lint pass.

  Fixed along the way: a visible line across the page where the faint background
  image ended.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec (S6 checklist with notes), README
  and session log are updated.

  Next: Screen 7, the Dream Archive: all your dreams in a gallery of artworks, a
  list view, and a "World" constellation of every symbol. Home's grid button
  and "See all" already link to it.

⏺ Screen 7, the Dream Archive, is built. It opens from the grid button and "See
  all" on Home.

  At the top:
  - a "This Month ⌄" filter, which switches between This Week, This Month and
    All Time;
  - your avatar;
  - the large title "Dream Archive" with a small count of dreams beside it;
  - a Gallery | List | World switch, with a highlight that slides between the
    options.

  The three views:
  - Gallery: your dreams' artworks in two columns of alternating heights, with
    the date and title on each. A feather badge marks dreams where you answered
    the reflection question.
  - List: white cards like the reference, each with the date, title, a 2-line
    preview of the dream, its symbol icons and the feather badge. The last card
    has paper layers peeking out underneath.
  - World: a map of every symbol in your dreams. Bigger circles appear in more
    dreams, and lines join symbols that shared a dream. Water sits in the middle
    as the most frequent. Tapping a symbol opens its Dream Echo page.

  Other behaviour:
  - All Time groups dreams under month headings ("September 2026").
  - The bottom bar has ✕ (back) · Search · ⇅ (Newest / Oldest first).
  - If a range has no dreams: "No dreams this week yet." with a Record a dream
    button.
  - Tapping a dream opens it, or opens Processing if it isn't finished yet.

  Tested:
  - Chrome: all three views, sort, the range filter (All Time shows month
    headings; This Week shows 7 dreams), a dream opening, and a World symbol
    opening its Echo page. No errors.
  - iOS simulator: all three views render correctly. The blue gear is Expo Go's
    developer button, not part of the app.
  - Typecheck and lint pass.

  Fixed along the way:
  - World view: the symbols overlapped at first. I spread them out over a taller
    oval and left space around the centre, so every label is readable now.
  - View links: links that choose a view (e.g. straight to World) were ignored
    if the Archive was already open. They work now.

  Not built: loading placeholders while dreams load. The data is local and
  appears instantly, so they'll come with Supabase.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec, README and session log are
  updated.

  Next: Screen 8, Search: find dreams by symbol, emotion or word. The Search
  buttons on Home and in the Archive already point to it.

⏺ Screen 8, Search, is built. It opens from the Search button on Home and in the
  Archive. On iPhone it slides up as a sheet you can swipe down to close;
  there's also a "Done" button for Android and web.

  Before you type:
  - Symbols: chips with each symbol's icon, name and how many dreams it's in
    (Water 4, Night 3…). It shows the top 8, with "All 15" to see the rest.
    Tapping a chip opens that symbol's Dream Echo page.
  - Emotions: pills with counts (Wonder 2, Calm 2…). Tapping one searches for
    it.

  When you type, results update live. You can search by:
  - dream title, symbol, emotion or theme;
  - words from your dream or your insight;
  - dates, e.g. "september", "monday" or "24 sep".

  Each result shows the artwork, the date, where it matched ("Symbol", "Your
  dream"…) and a snippet with your search words in bold white. Tapping it opens
  the dream. With no matches you get: "Nothing yet. Maybe you haven't dreamt it
  yet."

  The search box sits at the bottom, above the keyboard, with a clear ✕.

  Tested in Chrome, with no errors:

  ┌─────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Search    │             Result              │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ water       │ 4 dreams                        │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ black water │ 1 dream (matched in Your dream) │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ september   │ 8 dreams                        │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ dragons     │ the "Nothing yet" message       │
  ├─────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Calm chip   │ 3 dreams                        │
  └─────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┘

  Tapping the Water chip opens its Echo page, and tapping a result opens the
  dream. On the iOS simulator it opens as a native sheet. Typecheck and lint
  pass.

  I haven't checked that the search box stays above the on-screen keyboard; the
  simulator used the Mac keyboard.

  Fixed along the way:
  - results showed "Water water · Light light" because internal symbol names
    leaked into the text;
  - the Symbols and Emotions sections were squashed together;
  - a pointless "0 dreams" line appeared above the no-results message.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec, README and session log are
  updated.

  Remaining: Screen 9, Patterns, which is optional (your avatar already links to
  it), and Screen 10, Settings, also optional. After those, or instead of them
  if time is short, the next big step is the Supabase backend and the real AI
  for transcription, analysis and artwork. Which would you like next?

  ⎿  Created src/lib/patterns.ts (+59 -0)
       1 +import type { Dream } from '@/types/dream';                    
       2 +                                                               
       3 +export type Ranked = { key: string; label: string; count: numbe
         +r; share: number };                                            
       4 +                                                               
       5 +function rank(items: { key: string; label: string }[], total: n
         +umber): Ranked[] {                                             
       6 +  const map = new Map<string, Ranked>();                       
       7 +  for (const i of items) {                                     
       8 +    const prev = map.get(i.key);                               
       9 +    map.set(i.key, { ...i, count: (prev?.count ?? 0) + 1, share
         +: 0 });                                                        
      10 +  }                                                            
      11 +  return [...map.values()]                                     
      12 +    .map((r) => ({ ...r, share: total ? r.count / total : 0 }))
      13 +    .sort((a, b) => b.count - a.count);                        
      14 +}                                                              
      15 +                                                               
      16 +/** The simple personal summary behind S9. Only *ready* dreams 
         +count. */                                                      
      17 +export function dreamPatterns(all: Dream[], now = new Date()) {

      22 +    return t.getFullYear() === now.getFullYear() && t.getMonth(

⏺ Screen 9, Dream Patterns, is built. It opens when you tap your avatar on Home
  or in the Archive.

  It switches to a light lavender-to-white theme, as in reference R24, to signal
  "this is about you", and the iPhone status bar turns dark to match.

  Top to bottom:
  - Header: back · "Your patterns" · a settings gear.
  - Your photo (large) and Abbas.
  - A 2×2 grid:
    - Most felt: Wonder 25% · Unease 25% · Recognition 25% (the share of your
      dreams with each emotion)
    - Most recurring symbol: Water ×4 · Night ×3 · Light ×2, with icons. Tapping
      one opens its Dream Echo page.
    - Theme in focus: Transition, in 2 dreams
    - Dreams in September: a large 8
  - Dream rhythm: a simple bar for each day of the month showing when you
    remembered dreams. Days still to come are faint.
  - Footer: "8 dreams remembered since September 2026 · 2 reflections written".
  - With fewer than 3 dreams, the screen shows only "Patterns appear after a few
    dreams. Keep going."

  As your brief asked, it's a short personal summary, not an analytics
  dashboard.

  Tested:
  - Chrome: the stats are correct for the sample dreams, tapping Water opens its
    Echo page, and the gear opens Settings. No errors.
  - iOS simulator: renders natively with the dark status bar.
  - Typecheck and lint pass.

  Fixed along the way:
  - "Recognition" was cut off.
  - The bars were all full height and looked heavy, since each day had exactly
    one dream. They're now scaled so one dream is a calmer, partial bar.

  The settings gear opens a placeholder for now. Screenshots are in
  docs/screens/. The spec, README and session log are updated.

  What's left:
  - Screen 10, Settings (optional): profile, morning reminder, export my dreams,
    about, sign out.
  - Backend: Supabase plus the real AI for transcription, analysis, artwork and
    voice, replacing the mock. This is required by the assignment (Supabase, and
    AI for text, images and audio).

  Given the 24-hour deadline, I'd do the backend next and keep Settings minimal.
  Which do you want?

⏺ Screen 10, Settings, is built, so all 10 screens now exist. One gap: I
  couldn't confirm that the morning reminder actually gets scheduled on iPhone
  (details below).

  Settings opens from the gear on Patterns and uses the same light lavender
  style. Every row works; none are placeholders:

  Row: Your name
  What it does: Tap to edit, then Save. The Home greeting updates and the name
  is
  kept after restarting the app. Your name used to be hard-coded; it's now a
  saved setting.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Row: Remind me each morning
  What it does: A switch plus time choices (06:00–08:30). It schedules a daily
  phone notification: "What do you remember? Catch your dream before it fades."
   It works in Expo Go. If you refuse notification permission, a message
  explains how to turn it on.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Row: Export my dreams
  What it does: Opens the phone's share sheet with all your dreams as text:
  title, date, emotions, symbols, transcript and insight.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Row: About Dreamify
  What it does: Expands a short description and the credit for the orb library.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Row: Sign out
  What it does: Asks first, then clears the reminder, resets your name and goes
  back to onboarding. This is a stand-in until Supabase accounts exist.

  The app version is shown at the bottom.

  Tested:
  - Chrome:
    - renaming to "Zaeem" changes the Home greeting and survives a reload;
    - About expands;
    - Sign out goes back to onboarding and the name resets to Abbas;
    - on web, the reminder switch shows "Reminders work in the mobile app".
    - No errors.
  - iOS simulator: turning the reminder on brought up the real iPhone permission
    prompt, so that step works natively. But macOS won't let me tap Allow, so I
    couldn't confirm the daily notification was scheduled. Please turn it on
    once on your phone to check. That prompt is still open on your simulator;
    you can tap Allow or Don't Allow.
  - Typecheck and lint pass, and the iOS build compiles.

  Screenshots are in docs/screens/. The spec, README and session log are
  updated.

  ⎿  Created supabase/functions/_shared/deepgram.ts (+42 -0)
       1 +// Deepgram: speech-to-text (nova-3) and text-to-speech (Aura-2
         +).                                                             
       2 +                                                               
       3 +const API = 'https://api.deepgram.com/v1';                     
       4 +const STT_MODEL = () => Deno.env.get('DEEPGRAM_STT_MODEL') ?? '
         +nova-3';                                                       
       5 +// Aura-2 "Athena": calm, smooth — suits reading a reflective q
         +uestion.                                                       
       6 +const TTS_VOICE = () => Deno.env.get('DEEPGRAM_TTS_VOICE') ?? '
         +aura-2-athena-en';                                             
       7 +                                                               
       8 +function auth() {                                              
       9 +  const key = Deno.env.get('DEEPGRAM_API_KEY');                
      10 +  if (!key) throw new Error('DEEPGRAM_API_KEY is not set');    
      11 +  return `Token ${key}`;                                       
      12 +}                                                              
      13 +                                                               
      14 +/** Pre-recorded transcription of raw audio bytes. */          
      15 +export async function transcribeAudio(audio: ArrayBuffer, conte
         +ntType: string): Promise<string> {                             
      16 +  const params = new URLSearchParams({                         
      17 +    model: STT_MODEL(),                                        
      18 +    smart_format: 'true',                                      
      19 +    punctuate: 'true',                                         
      20 +    language: 'en',                                            
      21 +  });                                                          
      22 +  const res = await fetch(`${API}/listen?${params}`, {         
      23 +    method: 'POST',

       5 +## How it works                                                
       6 +                                                               
       7 +```text                                                        
       8 +App (Expo)                           Supabase                  
         +                 AI                                            
       9 +─────────                            ────────                  
         +                 ──                                            
      10 +signInAnonymously() ───────────────▶ Auth (anonymous user, priv
         +ate data)                                                      
      11 +record → upload .m4a ──────────────▶ Storage  dream-audio/<user
         +>/<dream>.m4a                                                  
      12 +insert dreams row (processing) ────▶ Postgres public.dreams  (R
         +LS: own rows only)                                             
      13 +invoke process-dream ──────────────▶ Edge Function (replies 202
         +, keeps working):                                              
      14 +                                       1. download audio ──────
         +────────────────▶ Deepgram nova-3 (STT)                        
      15 +                                       2. transcript + earlier 
         +symbols ────────▶ Gemini (JSON analysis)                       
      16 +                                          → title, emotions, sy
         +mbols (30-key icon vocabulary),

         +ion, question, art prompt, colour                              
      18 +                                       3. in parallel:         
      19 +                                          art prompt + house st

      20 +                                          question ────────────
         +────────────────▶ Deepgram Aura-2 (TTS)                        
      21 +                                       4. upload art + question
         + audio, status = ready                                         
      22 +poll the row every 1.5 s ◀────────── processing_stage 0 → 4 dri
         +ves the Processing screen                                      
      23 +insight voice note → transcribe ───▶ Edge Function → Deepgram n
         +ova-3 → { text }

         +ights; text-to-speech for the "Listen" button on the question |

⏺ I've set up the backend in the repo, with Gemini for dream analysis and
  artwork and Deepgram for speech-to-text and text-to-speech. It hasn't run
  against a real Supabase instance or the real AI yet. I can't create a Supabase
  project from here, Docker isn't running for a local test, and there are no
  API keys yet.

  How a dream flows now:
  1. The app signs in anonymously, so there's no sign-up screen and each
     person's dreams are private to them.
  2. A recording is uploaded, or typed text is saved, as a new dream.
  3. The process-dream function takes it from there:
     - Deepgram turns the recording into text;
     - Gemini writes the title, emotions, symbols (limited to our 30 icons),
       themes, interpretation and question;
     - Gemini paints a 4:5 artwork in our house style, while Deepgram reads the
       question aloud.
  4. The Processing screen shows real progress as each step finishes.
  5. On the Reveal screen, a new "Listen" button plays the question, and voice
     answers in the insight card are turned into text by Deepgram.

  That covers the assignment's text, image and audio AI requirements.

  Security:
  - The Gemini and Deepgram keys live only on Supabase's servers, never in the
    app.
  - Each person can only read and change their own dreams and files.
  - The .env files that hold keys are git-ignored.

  Fallback: without a .env, the app runs exactly as before on the sample dreams
  and placeholder AI, so it always works for a demo. I re-ran the earlier
  browser tests in that mode and they all pass. The new server functions pass
  Deno's checks, and the app's typecheck, lint and iOS build pass.

  What you need to do. It takes about 10 minutes; all steps are in
  docs/BACKEND.md:
  1. Create a free project at supabase.com and turn on Anonymous sign-ins.
  2. Get a Gemini key (aistudio.google.com/apikey) and a Deepgram key
     (console.deepgram.com), and put them in supabase/functions/.env.
  3. Run npx supabase login, link, db push, secrets set, then functions deploy.
  4. Put the project's URL and publishable key in .env, then run npx expo start
     -c.

  Decision for you: I can test the whole backend locally now by starting Docker
  Desktop and running Supabase on this Mac. That downloads a few GB and takes
  several minutes. Without AI keys, the Gemini and Deepgram steps will fail, but
  it will prove the database, security rules, storage, sign-in and functions
  work, and that errors are handled cleanly. Should I start Docker, or would you
  rather create the Supabase project and send the keys first?

  assets/Dreamify.zip; the zip doesn't need to be committed, and the export
  should be moved into ai-logs/ as .md.
